Introduction to NTA Level 5 Practicum Guide 
Overall Aim of the Practicum Guide 
The practicum guide has been developed to g uide tutors 
to effectively train  students in clinical and practical areas 
to transfer the theoretical knowledge into practice. It 
provides an opportunity for students to practice newly 
learned skills under the supervision and support of tutors 
and clinical instructors. This practicum guide provides 
guidance on required areas for rotation, specific skills to 
be learnt and tasks to be completed. Monitoring and 
evaluation methods and tools are al so included. This 
guide also attempts to promote standardization of the 
training in the practical and clinical settings; to ensure that 
students in all NTA Level 5  have same competencies to 
be acquired for graduation. 
Layout and Organization of the Practicum Guide 
The practicum is organized into five major sections as 
follows: 
Section I : Overview of the practicum which covers the 
overall aim of the practicum and definition of terms such 
as ‘practicum’, ‘clinical rotation’ and ‘ Community Field 
Work. The overview section also describes the structure, 
practicum areas, prerequisite, advance preparation, 
monitoring and evaluation. 
 
Section II : This section describes the clinical teaching 
team which includes tutors, clinical instructors and 
students. The sectio n also covers roles and 
responsibilities of the team and addresses the 
patient/client as an important resource who plays a key 
role in the clinical learning of students. 
  
Section III : This section explains clinical competencies 
according to NTA lev el 5 , s ummary of clinical 

===== PAGE 8 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   8 
competencies, aims, clinical/practical learning objectives, 
clinical/practical opportunities and prerequisites.  
Section IV : This section contains a summary of all the 
practicum modules and their allo cated time per week, 
including s pecific guidance for NTA Level 5  Semester I 
practicum modules. Included in each module are the 
overall aim, clinical learning objectives, competencies, 
practicum resources, prerequisites, clinical placement, 
specific activities/assignments, end of practicum 
assignment, monitoring and evaluation, and checklists 
specific to each practicum module. 
Section V: This section contains specific guidance for the 
Community Field Work  that students will complete within 
Semester I.  
Section VI : This section contains summary of all the 
practicum modules and their allo cated time per week, 
including s pecific guidance for NTA level 5  semester II 
practicum modules. Included in each module are the 
overall aim, clinical learning objectives, competencies, 
practicum resources, prerequ isites, clinical placement, 
specific activities/assignments, end of practicum 
assignment, monitoring and evaluation, and checklists 
specific to each practicum module. 
Appendix: Overview of the Practicum Guide Session. 
This appendix is a session that tutors  can use to orient 
students to the practicum guide before going to the clinical 
areas.

Who is the Practicum Guide For? 
This practicum guide is intended primarily for use by all 
tutors and clinical instructors who will assist students learn 
in the clinical setting. 
How Should the Practicum Guide be used? 
The practicum guide is intended to be used as a tool by 
tutors and clinical instructors to support students learn the 
appropriate skills as stipulated in the curriculum. 
Each of these groups has specific res ponsibilities in using 
this practicum guide as follows: 
 
 
 
 
 
  

===== PAGE 10 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   10 
Health Training Institutions’ (HTIs) Responsibilities 
For an effective clinical teaching and learning, the HTIs 
should ensure: 
 Effective utilization of the National Guideline for Health 
Training Institutions and Teaching Health Facilities 
(Clinical Placement of  Health and Allied Sciences 
Students). 
 Availability of updated Memorandum of Understanding 
(MoU) with Teaching Health Facilities (THFs) to 
smoothen placement of students at the practicum sites. 
 All Tutors and Clinical instructors are oriented on  
practicum guides, assessment plans and practical 
procedure books. 
 There are adequate and qualified clinical instructors. 
 Tutors participate in patient management  and student 
teaching. 
 Proper utilization of Monitoring and evaluation tools for 
practical/clinical teaching and learning. 
 Early and timely distribution of Practical Procedure 
Books to students at the beginning of the semester. 
 Students have adequate clinical /practical exposure as 
indicated in the curriculum. 
 Availability of adequate recommended skills/ medical 
laboratory equipment, supplies and reagents 
 Effective utilization of skills/medical laboratory for 
teaching, learning and students’ assessment

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors

Tutors’ responsibilities: 
 Review the practicum guide to make themselves 
familiar with the layout and contents. 
 Conduct a session to the students in class before 
going to the clinical area or field visit; see Appendix I 
for the session on ‘Overview of the Practicum Guide for 
NTA level 5’.  
 Go through the respective practicum module with 
students, covering clinical learning objectives, 
competencies to be achieved and assignments to be 
done in the clinical setting to achieve the 
competencies.  
 Go through the practicum guide with clinical instruct ors 
emphasizing the roles and responsibilities of the 
teaching team. 
 Collaborate with clinical instructors to prepare all 
resources as indicated in the practicum resources 
section. 
 Provide assignments to students as indicated within 
the practicum guide to reinforce learning process 
 Utilize skills lab/ medical laboratory to ensure students 
acquire competences of rare cases 
 Utilize skills lab/ medical laboratory during assessment 
of some competences 
 Utilize checklists to monitor and evaluate skills that 
students have acquired throughout the practicum. 
  

===== PAGE 12 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   12 
Clinical instructors’ responsibilities 
 Utilize the practicum guide to enable students learn the 
skills in the clinical area and other placements.  
 Collaborate with tutors to prepare all resources as 
indicated in the practicum resources section.  
 Provide assignments to students as indicated within 
the practicum guide to reinforce learning process 
 Utilize checklists to monitor and evaluate skills that 
students have acquired throughout the practicum. 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
 The aim of monitoring and evaluation in this context is 
to monitor student progress in acquisition of skills. 
 Monitoring and evaluation will be done using checklists 
and end of assignment reports. 
 The checklist is a kind of task analysis descri bing what 
units of performance (step by step) are to be 
accomplished and the performance requirements 
associated with each step. 
 The checklist determines whether students follow 
accepted steps in accomplishing required criteria 
(tasks/steps), how accuratel y they perform them, and 
in what sequence. 
 Checklists shall be used to support the student to grow 
and develop towards desired levels of clinical 
competence instead of using it as a formal assessment 
tool.  
  

===== PAGE 13 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   13 
Instructions for Completing Checklists 
 Each ski ll checklist contains a column on the far-left 
side labelled ‘Standard’. The standards noted in this 
column relate to the overall procedure area. For each 
standard, there is an associated list of Criteria 
(steps/tasks) for students to complete in order to 
successfully meet the standard. 
 There are three columns in the checklist labelled: 
‘Observed’, ‘Not Observed’  and ‘Not Applicable. ‘For  
each criterion, instructors/tutors must tick ONE of the 
three boxes according to the guidance below: 
o Tick (√) ‘ Observed ‘if a student performs the 
criterion (step/task) correctly and in the proper 
sequence.  
o Tick ( √) ‘ Not Observed’  if a student does not 
perform the criterion (step/task) correctly; OR does 
not perform the task in the proper sequence; OR is  
not observed performing a task that is able to be 
performed given the circumstances.  
 Note: Sometimes a criterion has sub -criteria. If 
there are multiple elements to a criterion and 
ANY part of the task is not observed, then ‘Not 
Observed’ should be ticked. 
 For example, the criterion ‘Assembles 
instruments for giving injection’ has the following 
sub-criteria: 
- Syringe and needles 
- Gloves 
- Swabs etc. 
 These criteria are not to be rated individually, 
but rather collectively. Therefore, if one or more 
of the sub -criteria are not observed, then the 
whole criteria (Assembles instruments for giving 
injection) should be marked as ‘Not Observed.’ 

===== PAGE 14 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   14 
o For all ‘Not Observed’ criteria, the tutor/clinical 
instructor will note them in his/her supervision 
notebook and deliberate  efforts will be made to 
create opportunities for the student to continue 
practicing the skill under support and supervision. 
o Tick (√) ‘Not Applicable ’ if the criterion (step/task) 
either does not apply to a particular step/ task, OR 
could not be carried out by the student due to 
circumstances beyond his/her control ( e.g. if 
necessary, equipment is not available). 
o Write comments in the far-right hand column 
labelled ‘Comments’. Comments can be provided 
regardless of which box you tick. It is a space to 
include further information about the strengths or 
weaknesses of a student for each criterion; or to 
explain why a particular task was no t applicable 
during the observation. Remember to note 
comments in this column especially if you marked 
‘Not Observed’ or ‘Not Applicable’, as they will be 
useful for recalling details about the gap in skills or 
the reason why a given task/step was not 
applicable. The comments should be very specific. 
General phrases such as poorly done  should be 
avoided. 
 Note: The comments will be communicated to 
students immediately after observation in the 
clinical area, specifying learning problems and 
specific action pl an to remedy the situation or 
help the student to learn the skill. 
 Tutors are advised to develop a clinical profile for each 
student so that whenever a tutor/clinical instructor 
meets with a student for a learning purpose, the 
tutor/clinical instructor wil l note the performance, 
strength and areas in which the student still needs 
assistance. This will also be helpful when tutors/clinical 

===== PAGE 15 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   15 
instructors are required to write clinical performance 
reports of students. 
 There are certain criteria which cut across a lmost all 
tasks to be learned, including hand washing before and 
after each procedure, putting on the gloves and other 
‘Infection Prevention and Control’ (IPC) measures, 
confidentiality, providing privacy, and creating rapport. 
These may be seen as monoton ous or not directly 
relevant to a procedure, but are important for 
professionalism and preservation of patients’ rights. 
They need to be emphasized at every client/patient 
encounter. 
 After getting adequate practice of a certain skill, a 
student should ask the tutor/clinical instructor to assess 
his/her level of performance and sign the procedure 
book if the desired level of proficiency in that skill has 
been reached. 
 Prior to using checklists all stakeholders including 
tutors/clinical instructors and studen ts should be 
oriented to the practicum guides thoroughly. 
 If a checklist does not meet the learning needs of your 
students in that particular location, make a note in the 
feedback forms at the Practicum Guide section, 
discuss it with the school administrat ion. Such 
information will be useful during the review of the 
Practicum Guides.

NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   16 
Practical Procedure Books 
 Students shall be provided with Practical Procedure 
Books at the beginning of the academic year. The 
Practicum Guide has been harmonised with the 
Practical Procedure Book as a guide to performing 
most of the procedures listed in the Practical 
Procedure Book. Students should be encouraged to 
practice skills at all the times when in clinical settings 
and use both the Practical Procedure Book and 
Practicum Guide for the acquisition of skills. 
 The tutor or a clinical instructor assigned for 
signing the student Practical Procedure Book 
should only do so after being satisfied that he/she 
has coached, supported and observed the student 
performing a task. 
 In order to help the student, learn clinical skills more 
effectively tutors/clinical instructor must work with 
respective administrators to create an environment that 
is conducive for clinical learning, including creating 
opportunities for students to learn,  ensuring the 
availability of working tools, providing immediate 
feedback and being available to them as a mentor.

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   17 
Section I: Overview of the Practicum 
Overall Aim of Clinical Training 
The overall aim of the clinical practice is to expose 
students to the  entire range of activities in the health 
facility. While rotating through the different clinical 
departments, students will acquire relevant skills to enable 
them to provide or participate in the delivery of services at 
their level. The clinical training will be given over the entire 
instructional period of each semester. 
The Purpose of the Practicum 
The practicum is a clinical attachment intended to give 
students opportunities for supervised practical hands -on 
application of theoretical concepts studied i n class during 
the current or past semester. The practicum may be a 
brief activity or could last longer. 
Clinical Rotation/Placement 
This is a form of practicum where students are attached to 
a clinical area for a specified period of time in order to 
learn the intended skills. At the site they have more 
interaction with patients and staff and have some 
responsibilities. During the clinical placement, 
responsibility for teaching relies on both clinical instructors 
and tutors. The practical time has to be uti lized 
appropriately through organization of classroom and 
clinical hours for students to have enough time for 
effective acquisition of skills. 
  

===== PAGE 18 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   18 
Community Field Work 
Community fi eld work is an off -college educational 
experience provided by an institution  to their students 
which normally involves travel to a selected community 
setting. Community field work aims to provide students 
with opportunities to work at a community level for health 
promotion, so that they can gain experience with 
assessing community health needs, planning with the 
community, implementing a plan, and evaluating progress 
with the community.  
Structure of the Practicum Areas 
During NTA Level 5  students will have 13 hours per week 
in the clinical /practical setting during semesters I and 15 
hours during semester II. In order to successfully complete 
the practical training, students should demonstrate 
competencies in Internal Medicine I, Paediatrics and Child 
Health I, Obstetrics and Gynaecology I,  Health Promotion 
(Community Field Work)., Co unselling, Reproductive and 
Child Health, I Obstetrics and Gynaecology II, Surgery, 
Internal Medicine II and Paediatric and Child Health II.

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   20 
Prerequisites 
For this NTA level 5, students should have completed NTA 
level 4 modules and the required theoretical prepara tions 
in NTA level 5 . These are discussed in the respective 
sections for each area of practice. 
 
Advanced Preparation 
For all practical procedures that are possible, students 
should be introduced to the skills in class through 
demonstrations, simulations and role plays. 
 
Clinical Teaching 
Tutors and clinical instructors have the responsibility to 
supervise, guide and teach students in the clinical setting. 
They also have responsibility of documenting student 
progress by making appropriate entries or endorsi ng 
procedures completed in the students’ Practical Procedure 
Books and relevant checklists. The roles and 
responsibilities of different staff in student learning are 
highlighted in section II of this practicum guide. 
 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Students will be monitored and evaluated based on their 
performance of specific tasks using checklists and other 
tools. Tutors and clinical instructors are encouraged to 
acquaint themselves with clinical assessment and 
benchmarking criteria as stipulated in the NTA le vel 5 
Curriculum.

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   22 
Section II:  The Clinical Teaching Team and Their 
Roles 
Implementation of practical training requires the 
collaborative ef forts of tutors, clinical instructors and 
students. Each of these groups has different roles in the 
teaching and learning process. The relationship between 
members of the clinical teaching team and students is 
important. Tutors, clinical instructors and st udents must 
communicate clearly with each other during  teaching and 
learning process.  
Students will gain the most from their experiences when  
provided with opportunity/exposure to practice the tasks 
repetitively and when given constructive feedback . 
Students are not expected to know how to do everything 
perfectly at once, but rather, they are in the process of 
learning to improve upon their current skills and learn new 
ones.  
The clinical instructor or tutor will work with each student 
or a group of studen ts during the process of clinical 
learning and teaching. The tutor and clinical instructor will 
be responsible to monitor, evaluate, and give constructive 
feedback on students’ learning . Also, the  students will 
have the opportunity to seek guidance from an d provide 
feedback to the tutors and clinical instructors.

Roles and Responsibilities of a Tutor in Clinical 
Teaching 
The main role of a tutor is to plan and coordinate  the 
placement of students and teach students in the clinical 
settings. He/she will have the following roles in guiding 
students:  
 Ensure that students have completed prerequisite 
classroom sessions prior to starting the relevant 
practicum  
 Develop a curriculum cum rotation plan  
 Decide on the times during which students should go 
to the practicum

Roles and Responsibilities of the Clinical Instructor 
At every clinical placement site, clinical instructors should 
be identified to directly guide students achieve 
competencies in specific areas. The following are their 
roles and responsibilities: 
 Receive and orient students to the clinical area  
 Introduce students to patients and other staff 
 Assign students to specific tasks 
 Involve students during management and care of 
patients/clients 
 Work with the students to set daily clinical learning 
objectives and plan 
 Teach and guide students to learn the intended skills 
 Demonstrate and provide regular guidance to students 
 Give constructive feedback to students and complete 
student checklist based on participation in activities in 
different areas: 
o Giving constructive feedback means pointing out 
the things that the student does correctly as well as 
things for the student to improve on 
 Act as a good role model for professional conduct 

===== PAGE 26 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   26 
 Meet with students and tutors to discuss student 
progress and clinical challenges 
 Document student achievements in their Practical 
Procedure Books and assessment tools, e.g. checklists 
 Participate in student clinical monitoring and evaluation 
 Write reports and submit them to the institution on 
students’ performances and progress  
 
Roles and Responsibilities of Non -teaching Clinical 
Staff (other staff in health facilities) 
Non-teaching clinical staff in the different areas should 
facilitate student learning by assisting students to achieve 
their objectives. The non - teaching clin ical staff will be 
expected to: 
 Facilitate integration of the students into clinical sites 
 Provide students with relevant tools and materials 
 Show students the best ways of behaving in clinical 
sites 
 
Roles and Responsibilities of the Student in Clinical 
Setting 
Students are expected to show diligence in clinical areas 
and behave in a way that is consistent with ethical 
practice. Students will have the following roles: 
 Learn the clinical setting leadership, policies, routine 
and procedures 
 Learn clinical sk ills according to clinical learning 
objectives 
 Present patient summaries and case studies and other 
assignments to clinical instructors and tutors as 
relevant 
 Perform other duties as assigned by clinical 
staff/instructors at the relevant site

 Complete assignments and submit them to the relevant 
clinical instructor or tutor as appropriate 
 Maintain medical records correctly 
 Support fellow students in the clinical areas for 
effective learning 
 Share clinical learning experiences in classroom and 
clinical areas in case presentations and clinical 
conferences 
 Receive feedback from tutors and clinical instructors 
on progress and discuss/ask questions 
 Provide feedback to tutors and clinical instructors on 
clinical teaching and learning process 
NOTE: Other specific ro les will depend on the specific 
area in which the student will be placed. 
  

===== PAGE 28 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   28 
Patient/Client as a Resource 
Patient/Client is a very important resource and plays a key 
role in the clinical learning of students. He/she is a 
constant resource for students as so urce of information 
and students’ feedback. However, the patient/client has 
rights which tutor, clinical staff/instructor and student have 
to observe as they interact with the patient/client. These 
rights include: 
 Being treated with compassion, love and respect. 
 Getting correct information about his/her health 
conditions, prescribed treatment and procedures. 
 Complaining and appealing in accordance with 
established procedures. 
 The right to privacy and confidentiality. 
 Refusing services if they do not meet th e required 
needs and standards. 
 Accessing health services, facilities and information 
according to their needs. 
 The right for informed consent, self -expression and 
choice of care. 
 Knowing health facility rules and regulations that 
apply to his/her conduct as a patient. 

===== PAGE 29 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   29 
Section III:  Clinical Competencies According to NTA 
Level 5 
Introduction 
The NTA level 5 graduates (Clinical Assistants) are 
expected to work at primary health care facilities under the 
supervision of a Clinical Officer. He/she is expected t o 
assess, diagnose and treat patient s/clients, perform tests 
for common conditions, and mobilize various resources in 
the community to deliver primary health care services. 
Specific functions of this position include: 
 
 Perform clinical assessment to patients/clients  
 Diagnosing and managing all common diseases 
 Recognising and referring complex cases 
 First-line management and referral for 
emergencies 
 Encouraging adherence to prescribed 
treatments 
 Assisting in the provision of preventive and 
curative care 
 Detecting and referring the at -risk mother and 
child 
 Providing health education to pregnant women 
on Reproductive & Child Health (RCH), 
including Prevention of Mother to Child 
Transmission of HIV (PMTCT) 
 Providing PMTCT care, Integrated Managem ent 
of Childhood Illness (IMCI).  
 Applying principles of public health to promote 
gender, new born and child health  
 Delivering effective Behaviour Change 
Communication (BCC) messages to patients, 
families, and village groups 
 Promoting nutrition at the village level 

===== PAGE 30 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   30 
 Collecting and analys ing statistical data on 
diseases, and using this information to promote 
health in the community 
 Reporting all epidemics, communicable 
diseases, and other health -related events to the 
Clinical Officers and other local government 
authorities

According to NTA Level 5 
Principal Learning Outcomes 
By the end of NTA level 5 students will be able to: 
 Apply knowledge and skills of Medicine in managing 
medical conditions at the primary health care facility 
 Apply knowledge and skills of Medicine in managing 
new-born babies and children with common

facility 
 Apply knowledge and skills of Medicine in managing 
common surgical conditions at the primary health care 
facility 
 Apply knowledge and skills of medicine in managing 
common obstetrics and gynaecological conditions at 
the primary healthcare facility 
 Apply basic knowledge and skills of public health in 
preventing diseases and promoting hea lth in the

===== PAGE 32 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   32 
Aim 
The overall aim of the NTA Level  5 practicum is to enable 
trainees to acquire  basic knowledge and skills of health 
promotion and clinical medicine to manage common 
health conditions at primary health care level.  The 
following broad aims have been proposed for trainees to: 
 Be able to prov ide preventive and rehabilitative 
services 
 Be competent in diagnostic and curative services 
 Develop a professional career with the stronger base in 
clinical medicine. 
 Be aware of professionalism, legal and socioeconomic 
influences in the delivery of health care services. 
 Be innovative in newly emerging diseases and 
outbreaks 
 
Clinical/Practical Learning Outcomes  
By t he end of NTA level 5 practicum  students are 
expected to be able to: 
 Manage patients with medical conditions 
 Manage patients with emergency conditions 
 Perform clinical procedures in managing 
clients/patients 
 Utilize effective counselling skills in managing 
clients/patients 
 Utilize basic principles of public health to promote 
health 
 
 
 
Clinical Competencies/Skills 
 Specified in each module of this practicum guide 
Clinical/Practical Opportunities 

===== PAGE 33 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   33 
During NTA level 5  students will be oriented to specific 
clinical areas and will: 
 Carry out the skills acquired during NTA levels 4  
 Perform clinical procedures during management of 
patients 
 Manage Medical, Paediatrics, Obstetrics, 
Gynaecological and Surgical conditions 
 Provide counselling, preventive and control measures  
 Manage clients with medico-legal issues 
 
Prerequisites 
Before starting the practicum, students should have 
completed NTA level 4 and had started theoretical 
preparation in this level. Students can go to clinical areas 
at the same time as they are receiving didactic training. 
These exposures are related to the particular area 
covered in class.

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 5 Clinical Medicine      37 
Clinical Rotation 
During the clin ical rotation in medical ward and OPD 
students will get the opportunity to develop relevant skills 
through activities, assignments and demonstrations in the 
management of medical conditions/diseases. In each area 
they will  have an opportunity to observe an d practice in 
order to intended learning outcomes. 
 
Activity1: In IPD and OPD 
Instruct student to  attend at least ten (10 ) patients with 
different medical diseases/conditions. 
For each patient, a student shall:  
 Perform clinical assessment to patients  
 Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
 Determine appropriate investigations to be performed 
to patients  
 Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
 Provide preventive measures to patients  
 Share findings with the clinical team and fellow 
students 
Medical Diseases/Conditions 
Poliomyelitis, stroke, epilepsy, Diabetic Mellitus Meningitis, 
Tetanus,  Rabies, Stroke, Pneumonia, Bronchitis, 
Common Cold, COVID 19, Lung abscess, Upper 
respiratory infections (Influenza, Tonsillitis, sinusitis), 
Tuberculosis, Genital discharge syndrome (Gonorrhoea, 
Bacterial Vaginosis, Trichomoniasis, Vaginal Candidiasis), 
Genital ulcer diseases (Syphilis, Chancroid, Herpetic 
ulcer), Genital warts, Urinary tract infections, Urinary 
Schistosomiasis, Helminthiasis (Ascariasis, Enterobiasis, 
Trichuriasis, Ancylostomiasis, Taeniasis, Strongyloidiasis, 
Hydatidosis), Diarrhoeal diseases (Dysentery, Cholera, 
Giardiasis, Isosporiasis and Cryptosporidiosis), Enteric 
fever, Hepatitis (A&E), Malaria, Trypanosomiasis, 
Septicaemia, Viral haemorrhagic fevers ( Yellow fever, Rift 
Valley, Ebola, Dengue), Anthrax, Brucellosis, Gastritis, 
Oesophagitis and Peptic Ulcers 

===== PAGE 38 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors 
NTA Level 5 Clinical Medicine      38 
Assignment 
Instruct students to make relevant documentation, prepare 
a presentation and share with the c linical team and other 
students for each patient. 
 
End of Practicum Assignment 
At the end of the In ternal Medicine, I rotation, instruct each 
student to  write a case report on one of the patients he/she 
presented. 
The report should cover the following: 
 Performed clinical assessment to patients  
 Established provisional and differential diagnoses 
 Order appropriate investigations and interpret results 
 Treatment given, 
 Follow up and outcome of the patient 
 Provided preventive measures to patients 
 
Assignment 
Instruct the student to submit the report  to the tutor for 
marking, grading and feedback. 
 
Note: The following seven procedures should be 
demonstrated in skills lab before students starts clinical 
rotation: 
 Airway suctioning 
 Assisting ventilation by ambu bag 
 Administering IV infusion in a required calibration 
 Inserting Nasogastric Tube 
 Removing Nasogastric Tube 
 Inserting Urethral Catheter 
 Removing urethral catheter

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   81 
CMT05103 Paediatrics & Child Health I 
 
 
Total Practical Time:  54 hours in 20 weeks of a 
semester 
Aim: The aim of the practicum is to give opportunity to 
students to manage children with paediatric conditions. 
 
Clinical Learning Outcome 
By the end of practicum, students are expected to be able 
to: 
 Manage children with paediatric conditions 
 Perform clinical paediatric procedures 
 Manage children by using i ntegrated management of 
childhood illness (IMCI) approach 
 Perform Emergency Triage Assessment and treatment 
(ETAT) 
 
Competencies/Skills 
 Perform clinical assessment to Paediatric patients  
 Establish provisional and differential diagnoses 
 Determine appropriate inv estigations to be performed 
to Paediatric patients  
 Treat, conduct follow up and refer Paediatric patients  
 Perform basic procedures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
 Provide preventive measures to Paediatric patients  
 Share findings with the clinical team and fellow 
students 
 Institute appropriate initial intervention in life 
threatening paediatric conditions before referral 
 Perform Emergency Triage Assessment and 
Treatment (ETAT)  


===== PAGE 82 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   82 
 Use Integrated Management of Childhood Illness 
(IMCI) approach in managing Paediatric conditions 
 
Practicum Resources 
 Practical Procedure Book 
 Clinical learning objectives 
 Clinical rotation plan 
 Clinical assignment sheets 
 Checklists  
 IMCI chart booklet, recording forms and mother’s 
card 
 All necessary equipment and supplies 
 
Prerequisites 
 None  
 
Clinical Rotation 
During the clinical rotation in Paediatrics and Child Health, 
students will get opportunities to develop relevant skills 
through activities, assignments and demonstrations in 
Paediatric wards, Outpatients and RCH clinics. In each 
area they will have an opportunity to observe and practice 
in order to acquire the intended competences 
  

===== PAGE 83 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   83 
Activity 1: Paediatrics and Child Health  
Instruct each student to perform an anthropometric assessment to 
10 children including: 
 Weighing, measuring height/length, measuring  the mid-upper 
arm circumference (MUAC), and measuring the head 
circumference 
 Interpret and Share findings with the clinical team and other 
students 
 
 
Activity 2: In Paed iatric/neonatal Ward, OPD,  TB and RCH 
clinics  
Instruct each student to attend at least fifteen (10) sick children 
(common paediatric conditions) 
 
For each patient, a student shall:  
a) Perform clinical assessment to patients 
b) Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
c) Determine appropriate investigations to be performed 
to patients  
d) Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
e) Perform basic procedures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
f) Provide preventive measures to patients  
g) Share findings with the clinical team and fellow 
students 
 
Common Paediatrics Condition 
Malaria, Dengue feve r, Meningitis, Tetanus, Common Cold, 
Diptheria, Bronchiolitis, Pneumonia, TB, Tonsilitis, Croup, Otitis 
Media, Diarrhoea Diseases, Impetigo, Leprosy, Pediculosis, 
Scabies, Taenia, Urinary Tract Infections, Malnutrition 
 
Assignment 
For each patient/client, instruct student to  make relevant 
documentation, prepare a presentation and share with the clinical 
team and other students. 
 

===== PAGE 84 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   84 
Activity 3: IMCI 
During IMCI training,  instruct each student to apply IMCI 
competences in management of sick children at the clin ical 
setting.  
 
IMCI steps: 
 Assess the child’s illness 
 Classify the illness based on signs 
 Identify treatment, 
 Treat the child. 
 Counsel the care taker 
 Follow up. 
 
Assignment 
Provide a test to students at the end of IMCI training basing on the 
above steps. 
 
Activity 4: Paediatric Procedures 
Before clini cal rotations divide students  into small 
manageable groups  (4-6) in skills lab  and train them 
regarding the following procedures: 
 Inserting and removing nasogastric tube 
 Performing urethral Catheterization 
 Performing phlebotomy 
 Administering intravenous fluid 
 Administering oxygen 
 
 
When students rotate in any of the areas for paediatric 
experience, e.g. paediatric/neonatal wards, OPD and clinics, they 
should carry out the following procedures as relevant to th e 
clinical setting:  
 Insert and remove nasogastric tube 
 Perform urethral Catheterization 
 Perform phlebotomy 
 Administer intravenous fluid 
 Administer oxygen 

===== PAGE 85 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   85 
 
End of Practicum Assignment 
Instruct each student to  write a case report on one  of the 
patients he/she presented. 
 
The report should cover the following: 
a) Perform clinical assessment to patients  
b) Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
c) Determine appropriate investigations to be performed to 
patients  
d) Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
e) Perform basic procedures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
f) Provide preventive measures to patients  
g) Share findings with the clinical team and fellow students 
 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Clinical instructors and tutors will make daily observations 
of students, provide support and document progress using 
checklist provided below. Clinical instructors and tutors 
shall a ssess, grade and give immediate feedback to the 
student for improvement. 
 
NOTE: Checklists of some tasks which have already 
been covered in previous NTA levels and modules 
should be obtained in their respective practicum 
guides/modules

===== PAGE 150 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   150 
CMT 05104 Obstetrics and Gynaecology I 
 
 
Total Practical Time:  45 hours in 18 weeks of a 
semester 
 
Aim: The aim of this practicum is to give opportunity to 
students to practice and manage patients with obstetrics 
conditions. 
 
Clinical Learning Outcomes 
By the end of practicum, students are expected to be able 
to: 
 Apply knowledge and skills of Clinical Medicine in 
managing pregnancy and labour 
 Apply knowledge and skills of Clinical Medicine in 
managing patients with gynaecological conditions 
 Apply kno wledge and skills of Clinical Medicine in 
managing patients with emergency obstetric conditions 
 Apply knowledge and skills of Clinical Medicine in 
managing patients with medical conditions in 
pregnancy 
 Apply knowledge and skills of Clinical Medicine in 
managing patients with puerperal conditions 
 Apply knowledge and skills of Clinical Medicine in 
performing obstetric procedures 
 
Competencies/skills 
 Perform clinical assessment to patients  
 Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
 Determine appropriate  investigations to be performed 
to patients  
 Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  


===== PAGE 151 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   151 
 Perform basic procedures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
 Provide preventive measures to patients  
 Share findings with the clinical team and fellow 
students 
 Perform speculum examination 
 Conduct normal delivery and post-delivery examination 
 Perform low cavity vacuum delivery. 
 Perform manual removal of placenta  (to be 
demonstrated at the skills lab) 
 Perform episiotomy 
 Perform repair of perineal tears 
 
Practicum resources 
 Clinical rotation plan 
 Clinical learning Outcomes 
 Assignment sheets  
 Practicum Checklists 
 Practical procedure book 
 All necessary working equipment and supplies 
 
Prerequisite modules 
 None 
 
  

===== PAGE 152 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   152 
Clinical rotation 
During the clinical rotation in Obstetrics ward, students will 
get opportunities to develop relevant competences 
through activities, assignments and demonstrations in 
respective wards, outpatients and RCH clinics. In each 
area they will have an opportunity to observe and  practice 
in order to acquire the intended competences. 
 
Activity 1: At Obstetrics Ward, OPD and RCH Clinic 
Instruct each student to  attend at least ten (10) patients with 
different common obstetric conditions. 
 
For each patient/client, a student shall:  
a) Perform clinical assessment to patients  
b) Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
c) Determine appropriate investigations to be performed to 
patients  
d) Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
e) Perform basic procedures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
f) Provide preventive measures to patients  
g) Share findings with the clinical team and fellow students 
 
Common obstetric conditions 
Hyperemesis gravidarum, Malaria in pregnancy, Genital tract 
infections during pregnancy, Urinary tract infection in 
pregnancy, Anaemia in pregna ncy, Asthma in pregnancy, 
Epilepsy in pregnancy, Diabetes in pregnancy, Abdominal pain 
in pregnancy, Vacuum delivery, Management of Normal 
puerperium, Puerperal pyrexia/sepsis, Puerperal mental 
conditions (Maternal blues, depression, and psychosis), Breast  
conditions in puerperium; Episiotomy repair 1 st and 2 nd degree 
perineal tears 
 
 
Assignment 
Instruct students to make relevant documentation, prepare a 

===== PAGE 153 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   153 
presentation and share with the clinical team and other students 
for each patient. 
 
Activity 2: Management of normal labour 
 Each student will monitor progress of labour for at least ten (10) 
clients using a partogram and conduct normal delivery of the 
same clients under the observation of a clinical instructor.  
Assignment  
Instruct students to make relevan t documentation, prepare a 
presentation and share with the clinical team and other students 
for each patient. 
 
  

===== PAGE 154 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   154 
End of Practicum Assignment 
Instruct each student to write a case report on one  of the 
patients he/she presented. 
 
The report should cover the following: 
a) Performed clinical assessment to patients  
b) Established provisional and Differential diagnoses 
c) Determined appropriate investigations performed to patients  
d) Treatment given, conducted follow up and referral of 
patients as appropriate. 
e) Performed b asic procedures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
f) Provided preventive measures to patients  
 
 Instruct students to submit reports  for marking, grading and 
feedback. 
 
 
 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Clinical instructors and tutors will make daily observat ions 
of students, provide support and document progress using 
checklist provided. Clinical instructors and tutors shall 
assess, grade and give immediate feedback to the student 
for improvement.

CMT 05105 Health Promotion 
Section V: Community Field-Work 
 
 
Total Practical Time: 70 hours in 2 weeks  
(7 hours per day, 5 days per week) 
  
 
Aim: The community field work aims at providing students 
with opportunities to work at the community level, and help 
communities to strategize for health promotion.  
 
Community Field Work Outcomes 
At the end of the community field work period, students 
are expected to be able to: 
 Conduct school health programs 
 Plan for integrated community health interventions. 
 Provide health education services 
 Provide health education services to Key Populatio ns 
on risk behaviours 
 Provide nutritional services to the vulnerable groups 
 Apply one health approach in preventing diseases of 
public health importance. 
 
Competencies/Skills 
 Conduct health education and promotion activities 
to the community. 
 Assess nutrition status of children in the village. 
 Perform simple disease surveys to the village. 
 Participate with health facility staff in managing 
facility wastes. 
Pre-requisite Modules:  
  
 None  


===== PAGE 188 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   188 
 
Practicum Resources 
 Community Field Work outcomes 
 Community Field Work plan 
 Community Field Work assignment sheets 
 Other necessary equipment and supplies 
 
Advance Preparations 
It is important that proper planning and preparation take 
place in advance before students are sent to the 
community so as to establish and maintain relationship 
with the community.  
 
Tutor’s roles for advance preparation 
 Liaise with the District Medical Officer ( DMO) of the 
respective district before going to the identified health 
facilities 
 Contact and meet with the health facility in -charge and 
the village leaders to orient them on: 
o Purpose and objectives of Community Field Work 
for students working with the community 
o Activities the students will carry out with the 
community 
o How the students will work and where students will 
stay if there is need for accommodation 
o Plan how the tutors will supervise and guide 
students in the community. The plan should 
indicate: Who, what, when and how to guide and 
supervise students in the community 
 Collect necessary information about the community 
and share with stude nts ( e.g. community norms and 
any additional information they need to know) 
 Discuss with the students the purpose, learning 
objectives and competencies for the field work 

===== PAGE 189 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   189 
 Instruct students to develop a plan of action to be 
achieved during the field work wh ich shall be 
presented in plenary and evaluated 
 Inform students the exact date and time of departure to 
and from the community 
 
Roles and Responsibilities in the Field 
 
Roles and Responsibilities of Tutors 
 Accompany and introduce students to the health facility 
and community leaders 
 Visit the students in the field for follow up and guidance  
 Document all activities done by the students and 
monitor progress 
 
Responsibility of Health Facility Staff and Community 
Leaders 
 Meet with the students to orient them a bout the facility 
and community 
 Work with students to assist them achieve their 
learning objectives 
 Provide necessary support toward attainment of 
learning objectives 
 Organize a meeting for students to meet the 
community members 
 Meet with students and tuto rs to discuss students’ 
progress and challenges 
Roles and Responsibilities of Students 
 Conduct community assessment based on community 
field objectives 
 Plan an intervention with the community 
 Implement the intervention with the community 
 Evaluate progress of the intervention 

===== PAGE 190 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   190 
 
Follow-Up after Community Field Work 
 Each group shall present and submit a community field 
report within one week after the attachment. 
 Write a letter thanking the health facility In -charge and 
community leaders. 
 Evaluate whether or  not the community field work 
accomplished the set goals. 
 File students’ group reports and tutors ’ supervision 
reports for future reference. 
 
Activity 1: Community Assessment 
 Divide students in small manageable groups and assign them 
to conduct a community  assessment of the assigned 
community. 
 Community assessment components: 
o Demographic data of the assigned community (population 
size, socio -economic and cultural activities, availability of 
health facilities and social services)  
o Assessment and identified community needs 
o Identified community resources 
o Suggested interventions 
o Community support received 
o Implemented activities 
o Community future plans 
 
 
 
 
Activity 2: Assessment of nutritional status of children  at the 
Dispensary. 
 Divide students in small manageable groups and assign them 
to conduct a simple survey to establish nutritional status of 
children ‘under five’ years of age using height/length for age, 
weight for age, mid upper arm circumference (MUAC) and 
weight for height. 

===== PAGE 191 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   191 
o Develop a simple data collection tool 
o Collect data  
o Analyse and interpret data obtained 
 Instruct students to share report  with the supervisor and 
community leaders. 
 
Activity 3: Simple Disease Survey to the Village 
 Develop a tool for data collection 
 Collect data related to disease in the community 
 Analyse and interpret data  
 Establish common diseases in the community 
 Give recommendations   
 Prepare report. 
 
End of Community Field Work Assignment 
Divide students in a group of 4 -5. They should prepare  report on 
the community field work experience.  
 
The report will cover the main assignments done during the 
community field work: 
 Community Assessment 
o Demographic data of the assigned community (population 
size, socio -economic and cultural activities, availability of 
health facilities and social services)  
o Assessment and identified community needs 
o Identified community resources 
o Suggested interventions 
o Community support received 
o Implemented activities 
o Community future plans 
o Nutritional status of children 
o Simple disease survey 
o Community responses and concerns 
o Challenges encountered by students and how they were 
solved 
o Lessons learnt and recommendations

This report will be shared with supervisors, community leaders and 
other students. 
 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Clinical staff at the health fac ility will make daily 
observations of students and provide support.  The clinical 
staff, tutors and community leaders will give timely 
feedback for students’ improvement.

NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   193 
CMT 05106 Counselling 
 
 
Total Practical Time:  36 hours in 18 weeks of 
a semester 
 
Aim: The aim of the practicum in Counselling is for students 
to utilize counselling skills to manage patients/clients at 
clinical setting.  
 
Clinical Learning Outcome 
By the end of practicum, students are expected to be able 
to: 
 Provide counselling services to HIV clients 
 Provide counselling to patients with common 
chronic illnesses  
 Provide counselling to terminally ill patients 
 Provide counselling to clients in crisis 
 Provide counselling to PWUD and PWIDs 
 
Competencies/Skills 
 Provide counselling to patients/clients 
 Conduct PITC session 
 Perform HIV test 
 Conduct post-test counselling 
 Link HIV infected  client to  care for continuum of 
care 
 Provide supportive health education and possible 
care to patients with common chronic illness (drug 
adherence, nutritio n, life style modification, side 
effects of drugs) 
 Provide supportive health education and possible 
care to a terminally ill patient (drug adherence, 


===== PAGE 194 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   194 
nutrition, life style modification, side effects of 
drugs) 
 Provide counselling to a client in crisis 
 Provide counselling to PWUD and PWIDs  
 
Practicum Resources 
 Clinical learning objectives 
 Clinical rotation plan 
 Clinical assignment sheets 
 Checklists 
 All other necessary equipment and supplies  
 
Pre-requisite Module: 
 None  
 
Clinical Rotation 
During the clinical  rotation in IPD and OPD, students will 
get opportunities to develop relevant skills in planning and 
implementing counselling sessions for clients/patients.  
 
Activity 1: IPD and OPD 
Assign students in small manageable groups of 4 -6 at IPD and 
OPD to perform the following tasks: 
 Observe four sessions of Provider Initiated Testing and 
Counselling (PITC), including pre -test counselling, testing 
procedure, interpretation of test results, and post -test 
counselling 
 Conduct four sessions of PITC  
 Refer clients to appropriate care and support services 
 Under supervision, fill in referral slips, and link clients 
appropriately 
 Record information in relevant administrative forms and 
registers 
 

===== PAGE 195 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   195 
Activity 2: HIV Care and Treatment Clinic 
During the HIV Care and Treatment Clinic (CTC) rotation, each 
student shall:  
 Observe five sessions of antiretroviral therapy (ART) 
adherence counselling (Note: students should observe both 
initial and ongoing/follow-up counselling sessions) 
 Under supervision, conduct three sessions of A RT 
adherence counselling (both initial and ongoing/follow -up 
counselling sessions)  
 Refer clients to appropriate care and support services  
 Under supervision, fill in referral slips, and link clients 
appropriately 
 Record information in relevant administrat ive forms and 
registers 
 
Activity 3: Paediatric Clinics 
During Paediatrics clinic rotation, instruct each student to:  
 Observe two sessions of pre - and post-test counselling in 
children  
 Conduct one pre - and post -test counselling session with 
children  
 Refer clients to appropriate care and support services 
 Under supervision, fill in referral slips, and link clients 
appropriately 
 Record information in relevant administrative forms and 
registers 
 
Activity 4: IPD and OPD 
Instruct each student to: 
Provide counselling to at least two client s with different chronic 
diseases (e.g. cancer, hypertension, and Diabetes) Mellitus) 
 
Activity 5: OPD and drug rehabilitation centres. 
 Instruct each student to  attend at least one client with 
substance use/drug abuse and do the following: 
 Assess the client using the ASSIST V.3.0 tool 
 Counsel the client to stop drugs abuse or substance use

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   196 
 Refer accordingly

Clinical instructors and tutors will make daily observations 
of students, provide support and document progress using 
checklists below. The clinical instructors and tutors will 
give timely feedback for student’s improvement.

been covered in previous NTA level or module should 
be obtained in their respective practicum guides.

===== PAGE 234 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine  234 
CMT 05207 Reproductive and Child Health 
 
 
Total Practi cal Time:50 hours in 20 weeks of 
a semester 
 
Aim: The aim of this practicum is to provide students with 
appropriate skills in providing reproductive and child health 
care services. 
 
Clinical Learning Outcome 
By the end of practicum, students are expected to be able 
to: 
 Provide focused ante-natal care (FANC) 
 Provide postpartum care to mother and infant  
 Provide family planning services 
 Provide comprehensive Prevention of Mother To 
child Transmission (PMTCT) of HIV services  
 Provide Adolescent Sexual and Rep roductive 
Health Services (ASRHS) 
 Provide services to Gender Based Violence and 
Violence Against Children 
 Provide screening and prevention services of 
Reproductive Cancers 
 Provide immunization and vaccination services at 
primary health care level 
 Provide new-born and child health care services  
  


===== PAGE 235 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine  235 
Competencies/skills 
 Perform clinical assessment to pregnant mothers  
(antenatal), mothers after delivery (post-natal) 
 Provide focused ante-natal care (FANC) 
 Provide postpartum care to mother and infant  
 Provide family planning services  
 Prevention of Mother to Child HIV transmission  
 Provide user -friendly services for adolescents and 
youths 
 Administer vaccines according to the national 
guidelines 
 Manage vaccines according to cold chain system 
protocols of TIVD  (Tanzania immunization and  
vaccine development programme) 
 Assess and monitor new born , child growth and 
development 
 Perform assessment of GBV and VAC survivors 
 Perform screening of Reproductive cancers 
 
Practicum Resources 
 Checklists 
 Practical procedure book 
 Clinical learning objectives 
 Clinical rotation plan 
 Clinical assignment sheets 
 All other necessary equipment and supplies 
 
Prerequisites 
 None 
 
  

===== PAGE 236 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine  236 
Clinical Rotation  
During the Reproductive and Child Health rotation 
students will get opportunity to visit variou s sections of the 
RCH clinic  (Dispensary, Health Centre and District 
Hospital to acquire relevant skills. In each area students 
will have opportunity  to observe and practice desired 
competencies relating to reproductive and child health 
services. 
 
Activity 1: At Antenatal Clinic 
Assign students to conduct the following activities: 
 Assess pregnant women systematically  
 Provide TD Vaccination 
 Screen clients for Gender Based Violence 
 Conduct PMTCT services: 
o Give pre-test information to a group of first -time antenatal 
clinic (ANC) attendees 
o Conduct HIV rapid tests 
o Provide pre- and post-test counselling for both HIV negative 
and positive results 
o Provide counselling about infant feeding options to a group 
of mothers, including HIV positive mothers 
 Record information collected onto the RCH 4 card and use the 
PMTCT register and HMIS monthly report forms 
 Refer patients to relevant areas for further management 
 
 
Activity 2: At Under-Five Years Children’s section 
Assign students in small manageable groups to conduct th e 
following: 
 Monitor growth and development and fill RCH card 1  
 Conduct immunizations as indicated in the TIVD 
 Screen children for Violence Against Children  
 
Activity 3: At Family Planning Section 
Assign each student to perform the following activities:  

===== PAGE 237 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine  237 
 Assess prior knowledge on FP 
 Counsel patients on Family planning methods  
 Establish client’s choice 
 Provide chosen family planning method 
 
 
Activity 4: Procedures at Family Planning Section 
Assign students to perform the following activities:  
 Provide oral and injectable contraceptives 
 Insert and remove implants 
 
 
 
Activity 5 : At Family Planning Section or Antenatal Clinic 
(ANC) 
Assign students to perform the following activities:  
 Conduct screening for breast cancer 
 Perform Pap s mear and Visual Inspect ion with Acetic Acid 
to screen for Cervical Cancer. 
 
 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
RCH clinic staff/instructors and tutors will make daily 
observations of students, provide support and document 
progress using checklists. RCH clinic staff/instructors and 
tutors will give timely feedback for student’s improvement .

NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   318 
CMT 05208 Obstetrics and Gynaecology II 
 
 
Total Practical Time: 60 hours in 20 weeks of 
a semester 
 
Aim: The aim of the practicum is to give opportunity to 
students to practice and manage patients with obstetrics 
and gynaecological conditions. 
 
Clinical Learning Outcomes 
By the end of practicum, students are expected to be able 
to: 
 Manage patients with obstetric emergencies  
 Manage patients with gynaecological emergencies  
 Manage patients with non-emergency 
gynaecological conditions  
 
Competencies 
 Perform clinical assessment to patients with Obstetrics 
and Gynaecological conditions 
 Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
 Determine appropriate investigations to be performed 
to patients with Obstetrics and Gynaecological 
conditions 
 Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients with 
Obstetrics and Gynaecological conditions according to 
guidelines 
 Perform basic proc edures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
 Provide preventive measures to patients with 
Obstetrics and Gynaecological conditions 
 Share findings with the clinical team and fellow 
students 


===== PAGE 319 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   319 
 Institute appropriate initial intervention in life 
threatening Ob stetrical and Gynaecological conditions 
before referral 
 
Practicum Resources 
 Practical procedure book 
 Clinical learning objectives 
 Clinical rotation plan 
 Clinical assignment sheets 
 Checklists  
 All other necessary equipment and supplies 
 
Prerequisites 
 None  
 
Clinical Rotation 
While students rotate at the Obstetrics and Gynaecology 
wards, OPD and Theatre they will be expected to acquire 
specific competencies related to management of 
emergency Obstetrics and Gynaecological conditions and 
non-emergency gynaecol ogical conditions. Students will 
be divided into small manageable groups as they rotate 
through different areas. 
  

===== PAGE 320 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   320 
Activity 1: Obstetric Ward and OPD 
Assign e ach student to attend at least ten (10) patients with 
different common obstetric emergencies. 
For each patient/client, a student shall:  
a) Perform clinical assessment to patients 
b) Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
c) Determine appropriate investigations to be performed to 
patients  
d) Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
e) Perform basic pro cedures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
f) Provide preventive measures to patients  
g) Share findings with the clinical team and fellow students 
 
Common obstetric emergencies 
Ante-partum Haemorrhage (APH), Hypertensive disorders of 
pregnancy, Postpartum haemorrhage (PPH), Prolonged labor, 
Obstructed labour, Shoulder dystocia, Cord prolapses, Fetal 
distress, Ruptured uterus,  Anaemia, Chorioamnionitis, 
Premature Rupture of Membrane (PROM), Pre-term Premature 
Rupture of Membrane (PPROM ), Uterine  inversion an d 
Retained placenta 
 
Basic Obstetric Procedures to be performed 
 Assist caesarean section deliveries 
 Lifesaving procedures in Obstetrics emergencies 
 
Assignment 
 Instruct students to make relevant documentation, prepare a 
presentation and share with the clin ical team and other 
students for each patient. 
 
 
Activity 2: Gynaecology Wards and OPD  
Instruct each student to  attend at least five (5) patients with 
different common gynaecological emergency and non -
emergency. 
 

===== PAGE 321 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   321 
For each patient/client, a student shall:  
a) Perform clinical assessment to patients 
b) Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
c) Determine appropriate investigations to be performed to 
patients  
d) Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
e) Perform basic procedures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
f) Provide preventive measures to patients  
g) Share findings with the clinical team and fellow students 
 
Common Gynaecological conditions 
Ectopic pregnancy, Molar pregnancy, Abortion, Ovarian torsion, 
Menstrual disorders and Pelvic Inflammatory Disease 
 
Procedures to be performed: 
 Manual Vacuum Aspiration (MVA) 
 Dilation and Curettage (D&C) this will be only demonstrated 
at skills lab. 
 
Assignment 
Instruct students to make relevant documentation, prepare a 
presentation and share with the clinical team and other 
students for each patient. 
 
 
 
 
End of Practicum Assignment 
Instruct each student to write a case report on one of the 
patients he/she presented. 
 
The report should cover the following: 
a) Performed clinical assessment to patients  
a) Established provisional and Differential diagnoses 
b) Determined appropriate investigations performed to 
patients  
c) Treatment given, conducted follow up and referral of 

===== PAGE 322 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   322 
patients as appropriate. 
d) Performed basic procedures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
e) Provided preventive measures to patients  
 
 Instruct students to submit reports  for marking, grading and 
feedback. 
 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Clinical instructors and tutors will make daily observations 
of students, provide support and document progress using 
checklist pr ovided. Clinical instructors and tutors shall 
assess, grade and give immediate feedback to the student 
for improvement. 
 
NOTE: Checklists of some tasks which have already 
been covered in previous NTA levels and modules 
should be obtained in their respectiv e practicum 
guides/module

43.3.14 Dilates the cervix to allow a cannula

slows down if the woman is in pain

CMT05209 Surgery 
 
 
Total Practical Time: 60 hours in 20 weeks of 
a semester 
 
Aim: The aim of this practicum is to give students 
opportunities to acquire relevant competencies related to 
practice and management of patients  with surgical 
conditions. 
 
Clinical Learning Outcomes 
By the end of practicum, students are expected to be able 
to: 
 Assess patients with surgical conditions 
 Manage patients with surgical conditions 
 Perform surgical clinical procedures 
 
Competencies/Skills 
 Perform clinical assessment to surgical patients  
 Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
 Determine appropriate investigations to be 
performed to surgical patients  
 Treat, conduct follow up and refer surgical patients  
 Perform basic procedures in relation to diagnosis 
and management 
 Provide preventive measures to surgical patients  
 Share findings wi th the clinical team and fellow 
students 
 Institute appropriate initial intervention in life 
threatening surgical conditions before referral

Practicum Resources 
 Practical procedure book 
 Clinical learning objectives 
 Clinical rotation plan 
 Clinical assignment sheets 
 Checklists  
 All other necessary equipment and supplies 
 
Prerequisites 
 None  
 
Clinical Rotation 
During the clinical rotation in surgical wards, OPD and 
theatre, students will get opportunity  to develop relevant 
skills in the management  of surgica l conditions. In each 
area they will have the opportunity to observe, practice 
and acquire different skills. 
 
Activity 1: Surgical ward and OPD 
Instruct each student to attend at least ten (10) patients with 
different surgical conditions. 
For each patient, a student shall:  
 Perform clinical assessment to patients 
 Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
 Determine appropriate investigations to be performed to 
patients  
 Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
 Perform basic procedures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
 Provide preventive measures to patients  
 Share findings with the clinical team and fellow students 
 
 
 
Surgical Conditions 

===== PAGE 335 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   335 
Tension Pneumothorax,  burn injury, Intestinal obstruction, 
Acute appendicitis, Benign Prostatic Hypertroph y (BPH), 
Peritonitis, Testicular torsion), Fracture and Dislocations, 
Acute pancreatitis, Urethral stricture and Urine retention, 
Cellulitis, Abscess, Paronychia, Pyomyositis, Wounds and 
Ulcers, Hernias and Hydrocele, Rectal prolapse, Epididymo -
orchitis, Animal bites. 
Assignment 
Instruct students to make relevant documentation, prepare a 
presentation and share with the clinical team and other 
students for each patient. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Activity 2: Surgical procedures 
Instruct each student to perform the following procedures 
 Surgical and social toilet 
 Suture wound 
 Wound dressing 
 Immobilize fracture 
 Incise and drain an abscess 
 Administer local anaesthesia  
 Suprapubic aspiration 
 Male circumcision  
 Insert and remove urethral catheter 
 
End of Practicum Assignment 
Instruct each student to write a case report on one of the 
patients he/she presented. 
The report should cover the following: 
a) Performed clinical assessment to patients  

===== PAGE 336 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   336 
b) Established provisional and Differential diagnoses 
c) Determined appropriate investigations performed to 
patients  
d) Treatment given, conducted follow up and referral 
of patients as appropriate. 
e) Performed basic procedures in relation to diagnosis 
and management 
f) Provided preventive measures to patients  
 Instruct students to submit reports  for marking, gr ading and 
feedback. 
 
 
 
 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Clinical instructors and tutors will make daily observations 
of students, provide support and document progress using 
checklist provided. Clinical instructors and tutors shall 
assess, grade and give immediate feedback to the student 
for improvement. 
 
NOTE: Checklists of some tasks which have already 
been covered in previous NTA levels and modules 
should be obtained in their respective practicum 
guides/modules

wound is adequately apposed

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine  389 
CMT05210 Internal Medicine II 
 
 
Total Practical Time: 60 hours in 20 weeks of 
a semester 
 
Aim: The aim of the practicum i s to give students 
opportunity to achieve the competencies to practice and 
manage patients with medical conditions. 
 
Clinical Learning Outcome  
By the end of practicum, students are expected to be able 
to: 
 Assess patients with medical conditions 
 Manage patients with medical emergencies 
 Manage patients with non-communicable diseases 
 Manage patients with Tuberculosis  
 Manage patients with TB/HIV co-infection 
 Provide Anti -retroviral therapy to patient with 
HIV/AIDS 
 Manage oppor tunistic infections in a patient with 
HIV and AIDS  
 Apply different strategies to prevent HIV and TB 
infections 
 
Competencies/Skills 
 Perform clinical assessment to patients  
 Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
 Determine appropriate investigati ons to be 
performed to patients  
 Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
 Perform basic procedures in relation to diagnosis 
and management 
 Provide preventive measures to patients  


===== PAGE 390 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine  390 
 Share findings with the clinical team and fellow 
students 
 
Practicum Resources 
 Practical procedure book 
 Checklists 
 Clinical learning objectives 
 Clinical rotation plan 
 All other necessary equipment and supplies  
 
Prerequisites 
 None  
 
Clinical Rotation 
During the clinical rotation in medical ward and OPD  
students will get the op portunity to develop relevant skills 
through activities, assignments and demonstrations in the 
management of medical conditions/diseases. In each area 
they will have an opportunity to observe, practice, and 
acquire different skills. 
 
Activity 1: Medical Ward and Outpatient Clinics 
Instruct each student to  attend at least ten (10) patients with 
different common medical conditions. 
 
For each patient/client, a student shall:  
 Perform clinical assessment to patients  
 Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
 Determine appropriate investigations to be performed to 
patients  
 Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
 Provide preventive measures to patients  
 Share findings with the clinical team and fellow students 
 Refer as appropriate 
 Make a follow-up plan  

===== PAGE 391 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine  391 
 
Common medical conditions 
Bronchial asthma, COPD (Bronchitis and Emphysema), 
Pulmonary embolism, Bronchiectasis, Hypertension, Heart 
failure, Filariasis, Hepatitis B and C, Onchocerciasis, Anaemia,  
 
Note: Diseases learnt in the previous semesters will  also be 
assessed.  
 
Assignment 
Instruct students to make relevant documentation, prepare a 
presentation and share with the clinical team and other students 
for each patient. 
 
Activity 2: Medical Ward and Outpatient Clinic 
Instruct each student to  attend at least five (5 ) patients with 
different HIV related conditions. 
For each patient/client, a student shall:  
 Perform clinical assessment to patients  
 Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
 Determine appropriate investigations to be performed to 
patients  
 Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
 Provide preventive measures to patients  
 Share findings with the clinical team and fellow students 
 Refer as appropriate 
 Make a follow-up plan 
Common HIV related conditions 
TB/HIV co-infection, HIV and AIDS, HIV Opportunistic infections  
Assignment 
Instruct students to make relevant documentation, prepare a 
presentation and share with the clinical team and other students 
for each patient. 
 
End of Practicum Assignment 
At the end of the Internal Medicine, I rotation, instruct each student 
to write a case report on one of the patients he/she presented. 

===== PAGE 392 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine  392 
The report should cover the following: 
 Performed clinical assessment to patients  
 Established provisional and differential diagnoses 
 Order appropriate investigations and interpret results 
 Treatment given, 
 Follow up and outcome of the patient 
 Provided preventive measures to patients 
 
Assignment 
Instruct the student to submit the report to the tutor for marking, 
grading and feedback. 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Clinical instructors and tutors will make daily observations 
of students, provide support and document progress using 
checklist provided. Clinical instructors and tutors shall 
assess, grade and give immediate feedback to the student 
for improvement. 
 
NOTE: Checklists of some tasks which have already 
been covered in previous NTA level and modules 
should be obtained in their respective practicum 
guides/modules

CMT 05211 Paediatrics and Child Health II 
 
 
Total Practical Time: 70 hours in 20 weeks of a 
semester 
Aim: The aim of this practicum is to give opportunity to  
students to practice and manage children with paediatric 
conditions. 
Clinical Learning Outcome 
By the end of the practicum, students are expected to be 
able to: 
 
 Manage new-born babies with neonatal conditions 
 Manage a child with HIV/AIDS 
 Manage children with paediatric conditions 
 Perform blood transfusion 
 Perform new-born resuscitation  
 Coach a mother to provide Kangaroo Mother Care 
 
Competencies/Skills 
a) Perform clinical assessment to patients  
b) Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
c) Determine app ropriate investigations to be 
performed to patients  
d) Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
e) Perform basic procedures in relation to diagnosis 
and management 
f) Provide preventive measures to patients  


===== PAGE 406 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   406 
 
 
 
g) Share findings with the clinical team and fellow 
students  
h) Perform rapid/quick patient assessment 
i) Perform lifesaving management 
j) Manage sick new-born 
k) Initiate and monitor KMC and refer as appropriate  
 
Practicum Resources 
 Practical procedure book 
 Checklists 
 Clinical learning objectives 
 Clinical rotation plan 
 All other necessary equipment and supplies 
 
Prerequisites 
 None 
 
Clinical Rotation 
During the clinical rotation in paediatric ward and OPD 
students will get the opportunity to develop relevant skills 
through activities, assignments and demonstrations in th e 
management of paediatric conditions/diseases. In each 
area they will have an opportunity to observe, practice, 
and acquire different skills. 
  

===== PAGE 407 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   407 
 
 
 
Activity 1: 
Assign each student to attend at least ten (10) patients with 
different common neonatal conditions. 
 
For each patient/client, a student shall:  
a) Perform clinical assessment to patients  
b) Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
c) Determine appropriate investigations to be performed to 
patients  
d) Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
e) Perform b asic procedures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
f) Provide preventive measures to patients  
g) Share findings with the clinical team and fellow students  
h) Perform rapid/quick patient assessment 
i) Perform lifesaving management 
j) Manage sick new-born 
 
Common Neonatal Conditions. 
Neonatal septicaemia, Neonatal meningitis, Neonatal 
pneumonia, Neonatal tetanus, Ophthalmia neonatorum, 
Omphalitis and Cord granuloma, Impetigo , Meconium 
Ileus, Essential  new-born care, Birth asphyxia, Birth 
neonatal jaundice, Haemorrhagic disease, Premature and 
low-birth weight babies, Haemolytic disease of the new -
born, Respiratory Distress Syndrome, New -born babies 
with birth injuries. 
 
Assignment 
Instruct students to make relevant 
documentation, prepare a presentation and 
share with the clinical team and other students 
for each patient. 

===== PAGE 408 =====

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors  
NTA Level 5Clinical Medicine   408 
 
 
 
 
Activity 2:  
Assign each student to attend at least ten (10) patients 
with different common Paediatric conditions. 
 
For each patient/client, a student shall:  
a) Perform clinical assessment to patients  
b) Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
c) Determine appropriate investigations to be performed to 
patients  
d) Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
e) Perform basic procedures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
f) Provide preventive measures to patients  
g) Share findings with the clinical team and fellow students  
Common Paediatric Conditions. 
Child with HIV/AIDS, HIV exposed child and child with 
HIV infections, HIV infected child with opportunistic 
infection, Bronchial asthma, Anaemia and Sickle cell 
disease, Diabetes Mellitus, Epilepsy, Poison, Acute 
rheumatic fever, Rheumatic heart disease, and Heart 
failure. 
 
Assignment 
Instruct students to make relevant documentation, 
prepare a presentation and share with the clinical team 
and other students for each patient. 
 
End of Practicum Assignment 
Instruct each student to  write a case report on one of the 
patients he/she presented. 
 
The report should cover the following:

Practicum Guide for Tutors and Clinical Instructors

a) Perform clinical assessment to patients  
b) Establish provisional and Differential diagnoses 
c) Determine appropriate investigations to be performed to 
patients  
d) Treat, conduct follow up and refer patients  
e) Perform basic procedures in relation to diagnosis and 
management 
f) Provide preventive measures to patients  
g) Share findings with the clinical team and fellow students 
 
 
Monitoring and Evaluation 
Clinical instructors and tutors will make daily observations 
of students, provide support and document progress using 
checklist provided. Clinical instructors and tutors shall 
assess, grade and give immediate feedback to the student 
for improvement.

Skills 
Observed 
Not Observed
